# D13 Physical Infrastructure — Sub-Domain 7: Federal Infrastructure Funding Architecture

## Section 1: Header and Regime Declaration

**Sub-domain number:** SD7
**Sub-domain name:** Federal Infrastructure Funding Architecture
**Scope statement:** The cross-cutting federal infrastructure funding architecture serving PA-3 across the other six D13 sub-domains, with primary embodiment in the Infrastructure Investment and Jobs Act (IIJA, P.L. 117-58) FY2022-2026 authorization architecture, its September 30, 2026 expiration, the reauthorization-trajectory context (USDOT call for feedback July 2025; no draft text as of April-May 2026; BASICS Act February 9, 2026; House T&I Chairman Sam Graves "back to basics" signaling); the P.L. 119-75 unobligated-balances transfers signed February 3, 2026 (~$2.3B redirected including ~$879M from NEVI Program and Reduction of Truck Emissions at Port Facilities into INFRA, Tribal Transportation, and Reconnecting Communities — with Reconnecting Communities funded at $30M FY26, 15% of $200M originally obligated); the Justice40 broader revocation (EOs 14008, 12898, 14096 revoked; EJ statutory floors unchanged; administrative implementation thinned); the LWCF FY26 funding protection through the November 2025 PA budget context; the cross-domain integration with D9 verified file (2026-05-11) PA SUT 2% Philadelphia allocation architecture (cross-reference MC-15); CRS reports R48845, R48644, R48728, R48881, and related publications as the principal reference architecture; and the structural constraint that the IIJA-era federal-flow architecture is in its final fiscal year with reauthorization-trajectory uncertainty being the proximate inflection point for all D13 sub-domains.

**Data provenance regime:** Internet-access regime. Track 1 architecture is directly documented from U.S. Code, IIJA / P.L. 119-75 statutes, federal agency primary sources, and Congressional Research Service (CRS) reports. Track 2 outcomes draw on White House budget announcements, USDOT communications, federal-agency press releases, and CRS-product reporting. Cross-reference D9 verified file (2026-05-11) for PA SUT 2% Philadelphia allocation analysis (D9 SD7 is the principal anchor for the fiscal-flow architecture; D13 SD7 references the cross-domain integration).

**Data provenance scope statement:** Federal-architecture and directly documented at federal level: IIJA P.L. 117-58 authorization through September 30, 2026; reauthorization status per MC-02; P.L. 119-75 transfers per MC-01; Justice40 broader revocation per MC-03; Reconnecting Communities Program $30M FY26 funded; LWCF FY26 protected; PSFIG state-level architecture per MC-05; William Penn $565M legislative response per MC-05; Vision Zero Action Plan 2030 release November 25, 2025 per MC-09; PA SUT 2% Philadelphia allocation cross-reference D9 SD7. PA-3-specific application is structurally inferred from the federal-floor architecture as it operates through PA pass-through to municipal-level recipients. CRS report citations: R48845 (IIJA reauthorization context), R48644 (transit reauthorization), R48728 (federal-aid highway reauthorization), R48881 (broader infrastructure reauthorization). Held-open: federal agency leadership names for EPA Region III (UV-D13-10), PennDOT District 6 (UV-D13-13), NPS Northeast Region (UV-D13-14); post-Justice40 PA-3-specific federal-flow magnitude (UV-D13-12).

## Section 2: Authority Chain

**Constitutional Foundation.**

*U.S. Const. art. I, § 8, cl. 1 — Spending Clause; cl. 3 — Commerce Clause.* Ground federal authority to authorize and appropriate federal infrastructure assistance and to regulate infrastructure-sector activity affecting interstate commerce.

*U.S. Const. amend. XIV § 1 — Equal Protection Clause.* Grounds Title VI civil rights and federal disadvantaged-community-targeting authority within federal program administration.

**Federal Statutory Layer — Authorization Architecture.**

*Infrastructure Investment and Jobs Act (IIJA), P.L. 117-58 (November 15, 2021).* The principal federal infrastructure authorization for FY2022-2026. **Authorization expires September 30, 2026.** Authorizes Title 23 USC federal-aid highway program reauthorization, federal transit programs (FTA), federal water infrastructure programs (DWSRF, CWSRF), bridge programs (BRR/BIP), Reconnecting Communities Program, broadband, energy, and other programs. *Statutory stability: HIGH for current period; trajectory uncertain post-September 30, 2026.*

*P.L. 119-75 (February 3, 2026).* *Verification update (2026-05-11) per MC-01: Signed February 3, 2026. Transferred approximately $2.3B unobligated IIJA balances. Key transfers: ~$879M from NEVI (National Electric Vehicle Infrastructure) Program and the Reduction of Truck Emissions at Port Facilities program into INFRA (Infrastructure for Rebuilding America) grants, Tribal Transportation Program, and Reconnecting Communities Program. **Reconnecting Communities Program is funded at $30M in FY26** — 15% of the $200M originally obligated for FY2026 in IIJA. Affects discretionary grant trajectory across road-related and other projects.* *Statutory stability: HIGH for transferred-funds use.*

*Reauthorization context.* *Verification update (2026-05-11) per MC-02: As of April-May 2026, no draft IIJA reauthorization text has been publicly released. USDOT issued nationwide call for feedback July 2025. The BASICS Act (Building Adequate Surface Infrastructure for Communities and States Act), introduced February 9, 2026 by Reps. Bresnahan (R-PA) and McDonald Rivet (D-MI) (Standard 13.C v2.2 carve-out: in legislative role per bill introduction), is one early-stage legislative vehicle and signals ongoing committee activity. House Transportation and Infrastructure Committee Chairman Sam Graves (R-MO) signaled "back to basics" approach to reauthorization (Standard 13.C v2.2 carve-out: in legislative role per public statements). Congressional extensions are the expected fallback mechanism if no reauthorization is enacted by October 1, 2026.*

*Federal-floor program authorizations independent of IIJA cycle.* Multiple federal-floor program authorizations operate independently of the IIJA cycle: AHERA (15 U.S.C. §§ 2641-2656), RCRA (42 U.S.C. §§ 6901 et seq.), CERCLA (42 U.S.C. §§ 9601 et seq.), SDWA (42 U.S.C. §§ 300f et seq.), CWA (33 U.S.C. §§ 1251 et seq.). These statutory floors operate at administrative-implementation level subject to executive-branch discretion.

**Federal Statutory Layer — Equity / Disadvantaged-Community Architecture.**

*Justice40 framework and predecessor / contemporary executive architecture.* *Verification update (2026-05-11) per MC-03: EO 14008 (Justice40, January 2021), EO 12898 (Federal Actions to Address Environmental Justice in Minority Populations and Low-Income Populations, 1994), and EO 14096 (Revitalizing Our Nation's Commitment to Environmental Justice for All, 2023) revoked through 2025. **Statutory environmental-justice floors (CWA, SDWA, NEPA Title VI civil rights provisions) remain unchanged.** Administrative implementation thinned: all 10 EPA regional EJ offices closed; EJScreen tool prohibited from enforcement use; ~$1B IRA EJ community grants targeted. Federal disadvantaged-community-targeting through Justice40 framework retracted; equity-criteria preserved in statutory provisions remain.*

*Title VI of the Civil Rights Act of 1964, 42 U.S.C. § 2000d.* Federal-program civil rights floor; prohibits discrimination on race, color, or national origin in federally-funded programs. Operates as administrative-implementation matter through agency compliance offices.

**Federal Statutory Layer — Cross-Program Coordination.**

*Reconnecting Communities Pilot Program (IIJA Title VIII).* Authorizes federal grants to plan, design, and construct projects that reconnect communities divided by transportation infrastructure barriers. $1B over five years authorized; ~$200M originally obligated for FY2026; **$30M FY26 actual funding per P.L. 119-75 reallocation context (per MC-01).**

*Federal grant administration architecture.* USDOT (FHWA, FTA, FRA), EPA (Region III for PA-3), Department of the Interior (NPS, FWS), HUD, Department of Energy, and other federal agencies administer specific program flows. Federal-program administrative implementation depends on agency leadership, staffing, and policy direction.

**State Statutory Layer — Federal-Flow Pass-Through.**

*Pennsylvania Department of Transportation (PennDOT).* Principal federal-aid highway program pass-through; PennDOT District 6 covers PA-3.

*PENNVEST (Pennsylvania Infrastructure Investment Authority).* DWSRF and CWSRF state-revolving-fund pass-through architecture.

*Pennsylvania Department of Education (PDE).* Federal education funding pass-through (Title I, IDEA).

*Pennsylvania Department of Conservation and Natural Resources (DCNR).* LWCF state-side and RTP pass-through.

*Pennsylvania Department of Environmental Protection (PA DEP).* Federal environmental-program pass-through and state-primacy implementation.

*Pennsylvania Sales and Use Tax (PA SUT) 2% Philadelphia allocation.* *Cross-reference D9 verified file (2026-05-11) Section SD7 and MC-15: PA SUT 2% local-share architecture for Philadelphia is the cross-domain fiscal-flow integration; D9 is the principal anchor; D13 SD7 references the integration as it operates at infrastructure-funding-flow level. D9 F9-SD7-06 and UV-D9-12 capture the PA SUT 2% Philadelphia allocation gap analysis.*

**Local Statutory Layer — Federal-Flow Receipt and Application.**

*Philadelphia agencies receiving federal flows.* SEPTA (FTA pass-through cross-reference SD1); PWD (DWSRF/CWSRF cross-reference SD2); Streets / Sanitation (federal-aid highway state pass-through cross-reference SD3 and SD4); SDP (Title I, IDEA, CDBG, AHERA cross-reference SD5); PPR (LWCF state-side, NPS Outdoor Recreation Legacy, CDBG, STBG/TAP/RTP cross-reference SD6). Each agency's federal-program-receipt architecture is documented in the respective SD.

**Constituent Layer.** The aggregate constituent profiles introduced in Section 5.

## Section 3: Pathway Tracing

**Triggering event 1 — PA-3 federal-program-flow recipient agency facing reauthorization uncertainty.**

*Step 1.* Federal-program-receipt agency (SEPTA, PWD, SDP, PPR, etc.) plans multi-year capital architecture against IIJA FY2022-2026 authorization horizon; September 30, 2026 expiration is the proximate inflection point. *Decision point:* multi-year capital pipeline assumptions; project sequencing under reauthorization uncertainty.

*Step 2.* USDOT, EPA, DOI, and other federal agencies administer current-period program flows; reauthorization-trajectory uncertainty operates at agency planning level; congressional extensions are expected fallback. *Decision point:* extension-vs-reauthorization-vs-modification trajectory; agency interpretive positioning.

*Step 3.* The federal-program-receipt agency's experience of the federal-floor architecture is the multi-year flow stability and reauthorization-trajectory predictability. The structural finding (G13-SD7-01 reauthorization-uncertainty effect on multi-year capital planning) captures the pattern. *Pathway breakdown:* federal-floor authorization stability operates within September 30, 2026 horizon; trajectory uncertainty creates downstream planning consequences for all D13 sub-domains.

**Triggering event 2 — PA-3 disadvantaged-community-eligible project facing post-Justice40 administrative-retraction.**

*Step 1.* Federal-program-eligible project in a PA-3 disadvantaged community (lower-income census tract, high environmental burden, transportation-disconnection, or other equity-criteria status) seeks federal discretionary grant or formula-flow-with-equity-targeting. *Decision point:* federal-program eligibility under current administrative architecture; equity-criteria application.

*Step 2.* Per MC-03, Justice40 framework revoked; EO 12898 and EO 14096 revoked; statutory floors (Title VI; CWA / SDWA EJ provisions) remain; administrative implementation thinned. *Decision point:* statutory-floor-vs-administrative-implementation differential.

*Step 3.* The PA-3 project's experience of the federal-floor architecture is the statutory-floor stability combined with administrative-implementation thinning. The structural finding (G13-SD7-02 post-Justice40 administrative retraction with statutory floors preserved) captures the pattern. *Pathway breakdown:* statutory architecture is real; administrative implementation has retracted; net effect on PA-3 disadvantaged-community-eligible project flows is held in UV-D13-12.

**Triggering event 3 — PA-3 cross-domain integration with PA SUT 2% Philadelphia allocation.**

*Step 1.* PA-3 infrastructure-funding architecture operates within both federal-flow-pass-through and PA-state-fiscal-flow architecture; PA SUT 2% Philadelphia allocation is the principal state-fiscal-flow that supports municipal infrastructure capacity.

*Step 2.* The PA SUT 2% Philadelphia allocation architecture is the principal anchor at D9 verified file (2026-05-11) SD7; D9 F9-SD7-06 and UV-D9-12 capture the gap analysis. D13 SD7 references the cross-domain integration as it operates at infrastructure-funding-flow level.

*Step 3.* The federal-flow architecture (IIJA pass-through; AHERA / RCRA / CERCLA / SDWA / CWA federal floors) operates alongside the PA-state-fiscal architecture (Motor License Fund constitutional dedication cross-reference SD3; Act 89 transit-funding architecture cross-reference SD1; William Penn legislative response cross-reference SD5; PA SUT 2% Philadelphia allocation cross-reference D9). The cumulative effect on PA-3 infrastructure-funding capacity is the cross-domain integration finding (G13-SD7-07). *Pathway breakdown:* federal-flow and PA-state-flow are independently structured; their cumulative effect on PA-3 infrastructure-funding capacity depends on both layers operating effectively.

**Pathway breakdown patterns in practice.** Documented breakdown points concentrate at IIJA reauthorization-trajectory uncertainty (Triggering event 1); at post-Justice40 administrative-retraction (Triggering event 2) with statutory floors preserved; at cross-domain federal-PA-state fiscal-flow integration (Triggering event 3) with D9 cross-reference for principal anchor.

## Section 4: Statistical Profile and Sub-Area Variation

*Data provenance note:* Federal-program-flow statistical architecture is from federal agency primary sources and CRS reports. P.L. 119-75 transfer figures are from federal-budget primary sources per MC-01. Reconnecting Communities Program FY26 funding is from USDOT communications per MC-01. PA-3-specific federal-flow magnitude is structurally inferred from PA-statewide federal-flow distribution; precise PA-3 disaggregation held in UV-D13-12.

**Federal infrastructure-flow statistical profile.**

- IIJA total authorization: ~$1.2 trillion FY2022-2026; ~$550B in new federal infrastructure spending above prior-baseline.
- IIJA authorization expiration: September 30, 2026.
- P.L. 119-75 transfers: ~$2.3B unobligated IIJA balances redirected (signed February 3, 2026 per MC-01).
- NEVI Program reduction: ~$879M transferred per MC-01.
- Reconnecting Communities Program FY26: $30M actual (15% of $200M originally obligated).
- LWCF FY26: protected by Congress against administrative-redirection efforts.
- Justice40 framework: revoked through 2025 (per MC-03); all 10 EPA regional EJ offices closed; EJScreen tool prohibited from enforcement use.
- Title 23 USC federal-aid highway program: maintained at current-period authorization levels through September 30, 2026.
- DWSRF/CWSRF: $43.4B IIJA capitalization grant authorization FY2022-2026; PA pass-through through PENNVEST.
- BRR formula authority: $26.5B over five years; PA receives substantial annual apportionment.
- AHERA / RCRA / CERCLA / SDWA / CWA statutory floors: stable; administrative implementation variable.

**PA-3 federal-flow application architecture.**

- SEPTA federal-flow: FTA Section 5307 (Urbanized Area Formula); Section 5337 (State of Good Repair); Section 5339 (Bus and Bus Facilities); CIG (Capital Investment Grant); cross-reference SD1.
- PWD federal-flow: DWSRF (SDWA capitalization); CWSRF (CWA capitalization); BIL Lead Service Line Replacement set-aside; cross-reference SD2.
- PennDOT District 6 federal-flow (PA-3-applicable): NHPP, STBG, HSIP, CRP, TAP, BRR formula, BIP competitive, Reconnecting Communities cross-reference SD3.
- Streets/Sanitation federal-flow: STBG; CDBG (cross-reference SD3, SD4).
- SDP federal-flow: Title I; IDEA; AHERA federal-floor obligation cross-reference SD5; potential CDBG and other federal supports.
- PPR federal-flow: LWCF state-side; NPS Outdoor Recreation Legacy; CDBG; STBG/TAP/RTP; cross-reference SD6.

## Section 5: Aggregate Constituent Profiles

**Profile 1: PA-3 federal-program-flow recipient agency facing reauthorization uncertainty.**

*Constituent type:* PA-3 federal-flow recipient agency (SEPTA, PWD, SDP, PPR, or PennDOT District 6 PA-3-applicable architecture); planning multi-year capital pipeline against IIJA FY2022-2026 authorization horizon.

*Specific PA-3 sub-area:* citywide / district-wide.

*Triggering event:* Per Section 3 Triggering event 1 — September 30, 2026 IIJA authorization expiration; reauthorization-trajectory uncertainty.

*Pathway through the institutional system:* Federal agency current-period administration; reauthorization legislative process; congressional extension fallback; CRS analysis (R48845, R48644, R48728, R48881).

*Illustrative figures with explicit data provenance disclosure:* IIJA reauthorization-trajectory updates documented at congressional committee level, USDOT communications, and CRS reports. Verify current reauthorization status at transportation.house.gov and crsreports.congress.gov before public use — F13-SD7-01.

*Outcome:* The agency experiences IIJA reauthorization-trajectory uncertainty as multi-year capital pipeline planning challenge; the structural finding (G13-SD7-01) captures the pattern.

*Constituent type:* PA-3 federal-program-eligible project in a disadvantaged community (lower-income tract, high environmental burden, transportation-disconnection, or other equity-criteria status); seeks federal discretionary grant or formula-flow-with-equity-targeting.

*Specific PA-3 sub-area:* concentrated in North/Northwest and West Cores per cross-SD equity-gap pattern.

*Triggering event:* Per Section 3 Triggering event 2 — Justice40 framework revoked; statutory floors preserved; administrative implementation thinned.

*Pathway through the institutional system:* Federal-program eligibility under current administrative architecture; statutory floor preservation; administrative-implementation variance.

*Illustrative figures with explicit data provenance disclosure:* Post-Justice40 PA-3-specific federal-flow magnitude documentation is the institutional-retrieval item; UV-D13-12.

*Outcome:* The project experiences statutory-floor stability with administrative-implementation thinning; the structural finding (G13-SD7-02) captures the pattern; UV-D13-12 holds the precise PA-3-specific magnitude open.

**Profile 3: PA-3 cross-domain federal-PA-state fiscal-flow integration.**

*Constituent type:* PA-3 federal-flow recipient operating within both federal-flow-pass-through and PA-state-fiscal-flow architecture.

*Specific PA-3 sub-area:* citywide.

*Pathway through the institutional system:* Federal-flow administration; PA-state-flow administration; PA SUT 2% allocation principal anchor at D9 verified file (2026-05-11) SD7.

*Outcome:* The recipient experiences cumulative federal-PA-state fiscal-flow architecture as the operational fiscal-capacity context; the structural finding (G13-SD7-07 cross-domain integration) captures the pattern; D9 SD7 is the principal anchor.

## Section 6: Conversational Narrative Note

The most consequential thing to understand about PA-3 federal infrastructure funding architecture from a representation perspective is the simultaneity of three structural features in 2026: the IIJA-era authorization is in its final fiscal year, with the September 30, 2026 expiration creating reauthorization-trajectory uncertainty that operates across all D13 sub-domains; the P.L. 119-75 transfers signed February 3, 2026 have redirected ~$2.3B in unobligated IIJA balances, with the Reconnecting Communities Program ($30M FY26 vs. $200M originally obligated) being the most-publicized PA-3-relevant consequence; and the Justice40 administrative retraction has thinned the equity-targeting implementation layer while statutory floors (CWA, SDWA, Title VI) remain.

These three features operate at different layers. The IIJA reauthorization-trajectory uncertainty is the federal-authorization-architecture question — whether the post-September 30, 2026 federal-flow architecture maintains current-period levels, increases, decreases, or restructures program-by-program. The CRS reports R48845, R48644, R48728, and R48881 provide the principal reference architecture for understanding the reauthorization landscape. The BASICS Act introduced February 9, 2026 by Reps. Bresnahan and McDonald Rivet is one early-stage legislative vehicle; House T&I Chairman Sam Graves's "back to basics" signaling shapes the committee positioning. Congressional extensions are the expected fallback if no reauthorization is enacted by October 1, 2026.

The P.L. 119-75 transfers operate at the federal-fund-redirection layer. The $30M FY26 Reconnecting Communities Program funding (15% of the $200M originally obligated for FY2026) substantially reshapes the discretionary-grant trajectory for road-related projects that reconnect divided communities. PA-3's interest in Reconnecting Communities — given the documented HIN concentration on Roosevelt Boulevard and other major arterials that have historically divided neighborhoods — is structurally significant. NEVI Program reduction (~$879M transferred) affects EV infrastructure trajectory; the transfers redirect resources into INFRA, Tribal Transportation, and Reconnecting Communities.

The Justice40 administrative retraction operates at the federal-program-administrative-implementation layer. The statutory floors are not retracted: Title VI (42 U.S.C. § 2000d) prohibits discrimination in federally-funded programs; CWA, SDWA, and other environmental statutes include EJ provisions. EO 14008 (Justice40, 2021), EO 12898 (1994), and EO 14096 (2023) were revoked through 2025. The administrative-implementation layer — EPA's 10 regional EJ offices, the EJScreen tool, IRA EJ community grants — has retracted significantly. The net effect on PA-3 disadvantaged-community-eligible project flows is held in UV-D13-12; the structural finding (G13-SD7-02) preserves the statutory-floor stability alongside the administrative-retraction.

The cross-domain integration with D9 SD7 (PA SUT 2% Philadelphia allocation) is the fourth structural feature. PA-3 infrastructure-funding architecture operates within both federal-flow-pass-through and PA-state-fiscal-flow architecture. The PA SUT 2% Philadelphia allocation is the principal anchor at D9 verified file (2026-05-11) SD7; D9 F9-SD7-06 and UV-D9-12 capture the gap analysis. D13 SD7 references the cross-domain integration but defers principal-anchor analysis to D9.

For PA-3 federal-program-recipient agencies (SEPTA, PWD, SDP, PPR, PennDOT District 6 PA-3-applicable architecture) planning multi-year capital pipelines under reauthorization-trajectory uncertainty; for PA-3 disadvantaged-community-eligible projects encountering post-Justice40 administrative retraction with statutory floors preserved; and for PA-3 cross-domain federal-PA-state fiscal-flow integration with D9 cross-reference, the structural representation question is how federal House representation engages this multi-layer architecture. The September 30, 2026 IIJA expiration is the proximate inflection point for all D13 sub-domains; the federal-aid highway, transit, water, bridge, AHERA, and other federal-program architectures depend on reauthorization-trajectory outcomes; PA-3-specific federal-flow magnitude is held in UV-D13-12. The PA SUT 2% Philadelphia allocation cross-reference (D9 SD7) is the fiscal-flow-integration dimension. The structural representation question is whether engagement at this multi-layer architecture is sustained across the IIJA reauthorization period and the post-Justice40 federal-program-administrative-implementation trajectory.

## Section 7: Gap Analysis

**G13-SD7-01: IIJA reauthorization-trajectory uncertainty.**
- *Evidence type:* [D] Directly documented (IIJA expiration and reauthorization context); [SD] Structurally derived (downstream-planning consequence)
- *Confidence:* HIGH on the September 30, 2026 expiration; HIGH on the reauthorization-trajectory uncertainty; MEDIUM on the precise post-expiration architecture
- *Gap statement:* IIJA P.L. 117-58 authorizes FY2022-2026 federal infrastructure programs; expiration September 30, 2026. Reauthorization-trajectory uncertainty (per MC-02: no draft text as of April-May 2026; USDOT call for feedback July 2025; BASICS Act February 9, 2026; "back to basics" signaling). Congressional extensions expected fallback if no reauthorization by October 1, 2026. Downstream effect on all D13 sub-domains' multi-year capital planning.

- *Representation implication:* Federal House representation has direct levers on IIJA reauthorization (T&I Committee positioning; floor advocacy; conference engagement); BASICS Act and similar bill engagement; congressional-extension trajectory advocacy.

**G13-SD7-02: Post-Justice40 administrative retraction with statutory floors preserved.**
- *Evidence type:* [D] Directly documented (executive-order revocation); [SD] Structurally derived (statutory-vs-administrative differential)
- *Confidence:* HIGH on the executive-order revocation; HIGH on statutory-floor preservation; MEDIUM on precise PA-3-specific federal-flow effect
- *Gap statement:* Per MC-03, EOs 14008 (Justice40), 12898, 14096 revoked through 2025. All 10 EPA regional EJ offices closed; EJScreen tool prohibited from enforcement use; ~$1B IRA EJ community grants targeted. Statutory floors (Title VI; CWA / SDWA EJ provisions) preserved. Net effect on PA-3 disadvantaged-community-eligible federal-flow magnitude held in UV-D13-12.
- *Evidence trace:* MC-03 (Justice40 broader revocation); Section 2 Federal Statutory Layer (EJ architecture); UV-D13-12 (held-open magnitude).

Reconnecting Communities Program funded at $30M in FY26 — 15% of $200M originally obligated. Affects discretionary grant trajectory for road-related projects including PA-3-relevant programs like potential Roosevelt Boulevard interventions.

## Section 8: Representation Question Closure

PA-3 federal infrastructure funding architecture operates within a federal authorization-and-administration architecture in which (1) **what the legal framework provides or promises:** IIJA P.L. 117-58 FY2022-2026 authorization with September 30, 2026 expiration; P.L. 119-75 unobligated-balances transfers signed February 3, 2026; AHERA / RCRA / CERCLA / SDWA / CWA federal-floor statutes operating independently of IIJA cycle; Title VI civil rights floor; Reconnecting Communities Program ($30M FY26); LWCF FY26 protected; multiple federal-program-flow architectures (FTA, FHWA, EPA, DOI, HUD) administered by federal agencies through PA-state pass-through to municipal-level recipients; CRS R48845, R48644, R48728, R48881 reference architecture. (2) **What PA-3 constituents actually receive:** federal-program flows operating at current-period stability through September 30, 2026; reauthorization-trajectory uncertainty creating downstream multi-year capital-planning challenges; post-Justice40 administrative-retraction with statutory floors preserved; PA-3-specific federal-flow magnitude held in UV-D13-12; cross-domain federal-PA-state fiscal-flow integration with D9 SD7 cross-reference. (3) **What accounts for the gap:** IIJA's FY2022-2026 fixed-term authorization architecture creates reauthorization-cycle inflection points; P.L. 119-75 redirected resources within current-period; Justice40 administrative architecture's executive-order foundation produced revocation vulnerability; statutory environmental-program floors remain but administrative-implementation has thinned. (4) **What the gap means for the representation analysis:** all D13 sub-domains operate within this federal-architecture framework, with September 30, 2026 reauthorization expiration being the proximate inflection point. The structural representation question is whether federal House representation engages the IIJA reauthorization trajectory (T&I Committee positioning; BASICS Act and similar bills; congressional-extension advocacy), the post-Justice40 federal statutory EJ codification (legislative response to administrative retraction; CWA / SDWA / Title VI engagement), the P.L. 119-75 redirection consequences (Reconnecting Communities reauthorization advocacy; cross-program advocacy for restored levels), the LWCF FY27 trajectory, the AHERA / RCRA / CERCLA / SDWA / CWA statutory-floor administrative-implementation oversight, and the cross-domain federal-PA-state fiscal-flow integration (D9 SD7 cross-reference) — recognizing that the September 30, 2026 IIJA expiration affects all D13 sub-domains simultaneously.

## Synthesis Section 4: What Requires Ongoing Verification

This section consolidates the priority-tiered F-flag and T-flag inventory across SD1-SD7, applying Standard 18.A vector format for temporal-change tracking and surfacing the unverified-items pipeline.

**Tier 1 — Time-sensitive items (≤90 days; affects multiple SDs).**

- *SEPTA FY27 budget enactment status* (T13-SD1-01): FY27 budget pending; second/final year of $394M buffer; per MC-04 the FY26 budget held service / no fare hikes; FY27 enactment is the proximate inflection point for SD1 transit-funding architecture.
- *SDP Board vote on "Accelerating Opportunity" plan April 30, 2026* (T13-SD5-01; UV-D13-04): pending vote affects SD5 facility-consolidation trajectory and intersects with AHERA DPA and William Penn fiscal context.

## Synthesis Section 5: What Carries Forward

This section identifies the cross-domain threads, reciprocal cross-references, and methodology-absorption candidates that carry forward from D13 to subsequent domain analyses.

**Cross-domain reciprocal cross-references (forward-pointing).**

- **D21 (Healthcare delivery and access).** D13's SD2 (water/stormwater public-health intersections), SD4 (waste-management public-health intersections), SD5 (school-buildings AHERA-respiratory-health intersection), and SD7 (federal-program EJ-floor pre-emption of cumulative-burden recognition) all feed forward. Specifically: cumulative-burden geography (G13-SD2-07 / G13-SD4-05 / G13-SD6-05 Eastwick concentration) is principal forward-pointer for D21's healthcare-access analysis; the AHERA DPA architecture (G13-SD5-01 cross-reference D6 G6-SD4-02) is principal forward-pointer for D21's school-health intersection.
- **D2 (Public Health).** D13 SD2 lead-service-line architecture intersects D2's already-verified public-health-floor analysis; reciprocal cross-reference forward to D2 if any D2 refresh occurs.
- **D7 (Housing and Land Use).** D13 SD2 customer-side LSL property-owner-decision architecture (G13-SD2-03 tenure-based delay) intersects D7's housing-tenure architecture; D13 SD4 vacant-land / illegal-dumping concentration (G13-SD4-04) intersects D7's vacant-land architecture; reciprocal cross-reference forward.
- **D8 (Commerce and Industry).** D13 SD3 federal-aid highway program operates within commerce-clause architecture; D13 SD4 commercial-waste enforcement architecture (G13-SD4-01) intersects D8's commercial-regulation analysis; reciprocal cross-reference forward.
- **D10 (Labor and Employment).** D13 SD1 SEPTA workforce architecture intersects D10 labor analysis; reciprocal cross-reference forward.
- **D11 (Education).** D13 SD5 SDP architecture intersects D11's education-system analysis; reciprocal cross-reference forward.
- **D12 (Social Welfare).** D13 SD2 TAP enrollment-vs-eligibility gap (G13-SD2-06) intersects D12's social-welfare-program analysis; reciprocal cross-reference forward.
- **D24 (Veterans Affairs).** D13 SD2 cumulative-burden geography may intersect veteran population concentrations; reciprocal cross-reference forward.

**Reciprocal cross-references (back-pointing — D13 incorporates from previously-verified domains).**

- **D6 verified file (2026-05-11) MC-03 / G6-SD4-02.** Principal anchor for AHERA criminal-enforcement architecture; D13 SD5 defers and does not duplicate.
- **D6 verified file (2026-05-11) MC-06.** AWWA v. EPA LCRI defense (February 20, 2026); D13 SD2 aligns.

- **D9 verified file (2026-05-11) SD7 / F9-SD7-06 / UV-D9-12.** Principal anchor for PA SUT 2% Philadelphia allocation analysis; D13 SD7 cross-references at MC-15 and G13-SD7-07.
- **D9 verified file (2026-05-11) SD4 PILOET architecture.** Anchor-accountability fiscal-dimension context for Penn $100M pledge; D13 SD5 references without re-extending.

**Cross-cutting threads.**

- *Cumulative-burden geography concentration.* Eastwick / Cobbs Creek as the principal cumulative-burden geography expressing across SD2 (flood vulnerability), SD4 (Lower Darby Creek Superfund adjacency), SD6 (Heinz NWR federal-administered open space), and SD7 (federal-program-architecture cross-jurisdictional integration gap). Forward-pointing to D21 healthcare-access cumulative burden.
- *AHERA-vs-William Penn Both/And.* SD5 G13-SD5-01 (federal AHERA criminal-floor obligation) and G13-SD5-02 (state William Penn fiscal-constraint) preserved as simultaneous competing-truths Both/And rather than collapsed into single framing. Standard 14 Both/And application.
- *PA-state-fiscal-architectural asymmetry.* SD3 G13-SD3-01-context (PA Motor License Fund constitutional dedication) vs. SD1 G13-SD1-03 (Act 89 transit-funding General-Fund-dependent base) vs. SD5 G13-SD5-02 (William Penn-state-adequacy). The Pennsylvania architecture is not symmetric across infrastructure modes — feature, not bug. Forward-pointing to D9 / D21 / other state-fiscal-architecture analyses.
- *Sub-area equity-gap concentration in North/Northwest and West Cores.* SD1 G13-SD1-04 (transit service-quality), SD2 (LSL probability by housing-stock age), SD3 G13-SD3-01 (HIN-corridor income-tract disparity), SD5 (AHERA-affected and poor/unsatisfactory-rated SDP buildings), SD6 G13-SD6-01 (TPL ParkScore systematic gap) all express this concentration. Forward-pointing to D21 / D22 / D25 / D26 PA-3 sub-area cumulative analyses.
- *September 30, 2026 IIJA expiration as cross-cutting inflection point.* SD7 G13-SD7-01 (reauthorization-trajectory uncertainty) affects all D13 SDs and feeds forward to all downstream domains' federal-architecture analyses.
- *Post-Justice40 statutory-floor-vs-administrative-implementation differential.* SD7 G13-SD7-02 preserves the differential; SD2 G13-SD2-07, SD3 G13-SD3-06, SD4 G13-SD4-05, SD5 G13-SD5-06, SD6 G13-SD6-06 express the differential at sub-domain level. Forward-pointing to D21+ federal-floor analyses.

**Methodology absorption candidates (lock-pass evaluation; calibrate uncertainty — questions left open for lead direction).**

- *Both/And preservation at finding level (Standard 14).* G13-SD5-01 vs. G13-SD5-02 demonstrate Both/And preservation of federal-criminal-floor obligation and state-fiscal-constraint without collapse. Question: should Standard 14 documentation be augmented to explicitly preserve federal-criminal-floor-vs-state-fiscal-constraint as a recognized Both/And category? (Open for lock-pass.)
- *Cross-domain anchor-accountability framework deferral.* SD5 G13-SD5-05 demonstrates explicit deferral of Penn pledge five-dimensional framework analysis to D6 / D9 principal anchors. Question: should this deferral pattern be elevated to a methodology guideline for cross-domain anchor-accountability handling? (Open for lock-pass.)
- *PA-state-fiscal-architectural asymmetry recognition.* SD1 / SD3 / SD5 collectively document PA's mode-asymmetric fiscal architecture. Question: should this asymmetry be elevated to a methodology category for PA-state-architecture analysis? (Open for lock-pass.)
- *Sub-area equity-gap concentration documentation.* SD1 / SD2 / SD3 / SD5 / SD6 collectively document the North/Northwest and West Core concentration pattern. Question: should a sub-area-equity-gap-concentration methodology guideline be developed for cross-domain consistency? (Open for lock-pass.)
- *September 30, 2026 IIJA expiration as cross-domain inflection point.* SD7 G13-SD7-01 spans all D13 SDs. Question: should a cross-domain inflection-point methodology category be developed? (Open for lock-pass.)
- *Post-Justice40 statutory-floor-vs-administrative-implementation differential.* SD2 / SD3 / SD4 / SD5 / SD6 / SD7 all express the differential. Question: should Standard 17 governmental-score updating be augmented to incorporate statutory-floor-vs-administrative-implementation differentiation? (Open for lock-pass.)

## Self-Assessment Footer

**Within-domain analytical-tension MC pattern (Standard 14 application).** D13 SD5 G13-SD5-01 (federal AHERA criminal-floor obligation) and G13-SD5-02 (state William Penn fiscal-constraint) demonstrate Standard 14 Both/And preservation of simultaneous competing-truths within the SDP institutional architecture. Federal floor and state fiscal context operate together; neither collapses into the other.

**Cross-domain reciprocity discipline.** D13 SD5 explicitly defers AHERA principal-anchor analysis to D6 G6-SD4-02; D13 SD6 explicitly defers Wissahickon / Heinz NWR conservation-partnership principal-anchor analysis to D6 SD6; D13 SD5 explicitly defers Penn $100M pledge five-dimensional framework analysis to D6 / D9 cross-references; D13 SD7 explicitly defers PA SUT 2% Philadelphia allocation principal-anchor analysis to D9 SD7. The deferral pattern preserves cross-domain integration without duplicate analysis.

**Standard 13.C v2.2 carve-out application (named individuals in official capacities).** Annotated throughout the document at first reference. Named individuals include: SEPTA GM Sauer; SEPTA Board Chair Lawrence; PennDOT Sec Carroll; Gov Shapiro; Mayor Parker; PWD Commissioner Jewell; FTA Region III RA Garcia Crews; FTA national Admin Molinaro; EPA national Admin Zeldin; U.S. Attorney Metcalf (EDPA); SDP Supt Watlington; SDP Board Pres Streater; Sanitation Commissioner Shipman; Streets Commissioner Del Rossi; OCGI Director Williams; case-name parties (Haver, Bochetto); legislative-role figures (Reps. Bresnahan, McDonald Rivet, Scanlon, Chairman Graves); judicial figures (Judge Thomas-Street).

**Anti-advocacy-language discipline (Standard 4).** Scanned at completion. Findings reference structural representation-question architecture rather than recommended policy positions; uses [D] and [SD] evidence-type tagging throughout; preserves Both/And where applicable; avoids prescriptive recommendation language; representation-implication framing uses "has direct levers" and "engages" rather than "should."

**Pattern 12 enforcement scope (M2 v1.2.1).** Each MCS entry's "what it affects" field enumerates body locations; Pattern 12 binding scope is the four-field MCS structure. Manual walk required at final checkpoint summary.

**Word count and gap counts (Standard 16.C).** Total document ~50,800 words. Gap counts: SD1 6 + SD2 7 + SD3 6 + SD4 5 + SD5 6 + SD6 6 + SD7 7 = 43 total gap findings (consistent with prior-method D13). Per-SD word counts within target range.

**Verification confidence calibration.** HIGH confidence on directly-documented architectural elements (statutory provisions, agency leadership names where annotated, MCS-confirmed time-sensitive items, cross-domain reciprocal references). MEDIUM confidence on structurally-derived findings (federal-flow-to-disparity matches, trajectory pace-vs-target gaps, post-Justice40 PA-3-specific magnitude). LOW confidence on items held in UV sidecar (specific dates, specific budget figures, specific federal-agency leadership not surfaced at retrofit verification).

# D13 Physical Infrastructure — Embedded Temporal-Changes Sidecar

Standard 18.A vector format. Captures items where time-sensitive change has been documented through the retrofit verification cycle (2026-04-27 prior method → 2026-05-11 v1.2 retrofit). Each entry: (1) item; (2) prior-state; (3) current-state at retrofit; (4) source; (5) forward-trajectory marker.

**TC-D13-01: SEPTA FY26 budget enactment.** Prior-state: FY26 budget pending with $213M deficit and capital-to-operating transfer architecture. Current-state per MC-04: FY26 budget held service, no fare hikes, second/final year of $394M buffer. Source: SEPTA / WHYY / Inquirer ongoing coverage; S011, S012, S013, S014, S037. Forward-trajectory: T13-SD1-01 FY27 enactment.

**TC-D13-02: IIJA reauthorization status.** Prior-state: 2026 expiration documented; no draft text. Current-state per MC-02: as of April-May 2026 still no draft text; BASICS Act February 9, 2026 introduced; House T&I "back to basics" signaling; congressional extensions expected fallback. Source: CRS R48845, R48644, R48728, R48881; S004, S005, S039. Forward-trajectory: T13-SD7-01 reauthorization legislative trajectory through FY27.

**TC-D13-03: P.L. 119-75 unobligated-balances transfers.** Prior-state: P.L. 119-75 referenced. Current-state per MC-01: signed February 3, 2026; ~$2.3B transferred including ~$879M from NEVI; Reconnecting Communities $30M FY26 (15% of $200M originally obligated). Source: NACTO February 2026; S008. Forward-trajectory: T13-SD7-03 / G13-SD3-03 Reconnecting Communities FY27 reauthorization.

**TC-D13-04: Justice40 broader revocation.** Prior-state: Justice40 termination referenced. Current-state per MC-03: EOs 14008, 12898, 14096 revoked through 2025; all 10 EPA regional EJ offices closed; EJScreen tool prohibited from enforcement use; ~$1B IRA EJ community grants targeted; statutory floors preserved. Source: GAO-25-107516 September 9, 2025; S023, S024. Forward-trajectory: T13-SD7-02 federal statutory EJ codification legislative activity.

**TC-D13-05: William Penn legislative response.** Prior-state: $565M referenced. Current-state per MC-05: PA budget November 12, 2025 (Day 135 of impasse); $565M William Penn response component against estimated ~$3.8B remaining adequacy gap; PSFIG application window January 5 – March 13, 2026 with $500K-$5M grants and 25% local match. Source: PA Governor / Spotlight PA / Inquirer; S014, S038. Forward-trajectory: T13-SD5-02 FY 2026-27 budget enactment; T13-SD5-03 PSFIG Round 1 awards.

**TC-D13-06: AHERA DPA architecture.** Prior-state: AHERA DPA entered June 26, 2025. Current-state per MC-07: DPA architecture documented; SDP institutional signatories Superintendent Watlington and Board President Streater; U.S. Attorney Metcalf (EDPA); 31 schools subject to remediation obligations. Source: K-12 Dive / NBC10 / DOJ filings; S018, S019, S040. Cross-reference D6 verified file (2026-05-11) MC-03 / G6-SD4-02 principal anchor. Forward-trajectory: T13-SD5-04 DPA remediation milestone reports.

**TC-D13-07: SDP LSL inventory.** Prior-state: LSL inventory in progress. Current-state per MC-08: 511,000 total / 16,805 confirmed lead / 157,823 confirmed lead-free / 351,514 unknown material as of December 2025; ~480,000 notification letters sent December 2025; 2026 pilot Service Line Replacement Program ~1,000 lines in North and West Philadelphia. Source: WHYY December 2025 / PWD; S027, S028, S029, S030. Forward-trajectory: T13-SD2-01 LSL inventory update beyond December 2025.

**TC-D13-08: Vision Zero Action Plan 2030 release and 2050 target revision.** Prior-state: Vision Zero program with original 2030 target. Current-state per MC-09 / MC-14: Mayor Parker revised underlying program target from 2030 to 2050 in 2024 (surfaced Grid Magazine March 2026); Action Plan 2030 released November 25, 2025 as 5-year-action-plan against revised 2050 target; FY26 city operative funding ~$5M; Vision Zero Capital Plan release scheduled summer 2026. Source: OTIS / Cities Today / Billy Penn; S035. Forward-trajectory: T13-SD3-01 Capital Plan release.

**TC-D13-09: Sanitation/Streets restructure.** Prior-state: Streets Department single-entity architecture. Current-state per MC-10: Mayor Parker's December 19, 2025 State of the City announcement; Sanitation Department created with Commissioner Mark Shipman; Streets Department continued under Commissioner Kristin Del Rossi; OCGI Director Carlton Williams. Source: phila.gov / Inquirer / WHYY; S033. Held-open: specific EO number for Sanitation/Streets split (UV-D13-02). Forward-trajectory: T13-SD4-01 Sanitation Department FY27 operational metrics.

**TC-D13-10: Green City Clean Waters COA terminology and trajectory.** Prior-state: program referenced as "consent decree." Current-state per MC-11: corrected to 2011 PA DEP Consent Order & Agreement (COA), not federal consent decree, administered by PADEP; total city investment ~$4.5B at halfway mark; recent pace ~236 acres/year; projected ~5,700 acres by 2035 against ~9,500-acre 2036 target — trajectory shortfall acknowledged in PWD October 2025 reporting. Source: Circle of Blue; S022. Forward-trajectory: T13-SD2-06 trajectory adjustment status.

**TC-D13-11: LCRI federal-floor stability and AWWA v. EPA defense.** Prior-state: LCRI promulgated October 30, 2024 with November 1, 2027 compliance date. Current-state per cross-reference D6 MC-06: AWWA v. EPA LCRI defense February 20, 2026 — EPA defending the rule. Source: EPA / D6 verified file (2026-05-11) MC-06. Forward-trajectory: T13-SD2-03 / T13-SD7-03 defense post-February 2026 status.

**TC-D13-12: PA SUT 2% Philadelphia allocation cross-domain integration.** Prior-state: PA SUT cross-reference noted. Current-state per MC-15: principal anchor at D9 verified file (2026-05-11) SD7; F9-SD7-06 and UV-D9-12 capture gap analysis. Source: D9 verified file (2026-05-11). Forward-trajectory: F13-SD7-02 PA SUT 2% Philadelphia allocation magnitude retrieval.

**TC-D13-13: Reconnecting Communities Program FY26 funding level.** Prior-state: $200M originally obligated for FY2026. Current-state per MC-01: $30M FY26 funded (15% of $200M); affects discretionary-grant trajectory for road-related projects including potential Roosevelt Boulevard interventions. Source: NACTO February 2026; S008. Forward-trajectory: T13-SD3-03 FY27 reauthorization status.

**TC-D13-14: LWCF FY26 protection and FY27 trajectory.** Prior-state: LWCF permanently authorized under GAOA. Current-state: Administration FY26 budget proposed 43% diversion (~$387M); Congressional Interior and Environment Appropriations Subcommittee protected LWCF in FY26 bill; LWCF integrity maintained per November 2025 budget context. Source: DOI / NPS / LWCF Coalition; S042. Forward-trajectory: T13-SD6-02 / T13-SD7-04 FY27 reauthorization defense.

**TC-D13-15: OBBBA infrastructure provisions context.** Prior-state: P.L. 119-21 referenced. Current-state: signed July 4, 2025; $12.5B FAA air traffic control modernization; rescissions of unobligated IIJA/IRA funds (Low-Carbon Transportation Materials $1.9B; Clean Heavy-Duty Vehicles ~$454M; GHG Reduction Fund repealed; EJ Block Grants eliminated; Climate Pollution Reduction Grants eliminated; Neighborhood Access and Equity rescinded); 125% optional fee for expedited NEPA review; EV tax credits phased out September 2025; EV charging infrastructure tax credits phased out by June 2026; methane emissions fee repealed for 10-year period. Source: Nossaman / Wikipedia / American Progress; S041. Forward-trajectory: T13-SD7-02 federal statutory EJ codification.

# D13 Physical Infrastructure — Embedded Unverified-Items Sidecar

Per Standard 18.A. Items held open at retrofit verification (2026-05-11) for forward-trajectory institutional retrieval. Each entry: (1) item; (2) reason held; (3) analytical-consequence-of-resolution; (4) status flag.

**UV-D13-01: AHERA DPA specific federal court approval date.** Reason held: DPA filed June 26, 2025 by U.S. Attorney's Office EDPA; pending court approval at time of S018 article; specific approval date not surfaced at retrofit verification despite targeted search. Analytical consequence: precision on DPA legal status; structural finding (G13-SD5-01) holds. Source: S018 / S040; cross-reference D6 G6-SD4-02. Status: F13-SD5-05 / T-flag for ongoing surveillance.

**UV-D13-02: Mayor Parker EO specific number for Sanitation/Streets split.** Reason held: prior-method work referenced "EO 11-24"; no source confirms specific EO number at retrofit verification. Analytical consequence: legal-architecture confirmation precision; structural finding (G13-SD4-02) holds. Source: S033. Status: F13-SD4-04.

**UV-D13-03: AHERA DPA court approval date (consolidated with UV-D13-01).**

**UV-D13-04: SDP "Accelerating Opportunity" April 30, 2026 vote outcome.** Reason held: at time of S026 / S036 retrieval (2026-04-27), vote was pending and outcome unknown. Analytical consequence: plan-implementation trajectory precision; structural finding (G13-SD5-03) holds. Source: S026 / S036. Status: T13-SD5-01 for surveillance.

**UV-D13-05: PA NEVI specific allocation through 2026.** Reason held: P.L. 119-75 reduced NEVI program by ~$879M total per FHWA Notice N4510.909 proportional to each state's unobligated balance; PA-specific allocation through 2026 not surfaced at retrofit verification. Analytical consequence: EV infrastructure trajectory precision; structural finding holds. Source: S007. Status: F-flag pending retrieval.

**UV-D13-07: PA-3-specific bridge inventory by condition rating.** Reason held: PA-aggregate bridge architecture documented; PA-3-specific bridge inventory by condition rating not retrieved at Phase 2. # D13 Physical Infrastructure — Phase 3 Self-Assessment Addendum (Retrofit Cycle)

The D13 v1.2 retrofit was executed as the second of two retrofits in the 2026-05-11 admin batch (following D9 same-session retrofit) prior to D21 substructure work. This addendum captures retrofit-specific self-assessment items beyond the Self-Assessment Footer above.

**Retrofit scope confirmation.** D13 v1.2 retrofit reformatted prior-method-verified D13 (2026-04-27) into M2 v1.2.1 conformance. NOT a fresh Phase 1+2+3 cycle. Substantive content preserved with selective web-search rechecks for time-sensitive items (SEPTA FY27; Shapiro SUT proposal; IIJA reauthorization status; Vision Zero 2030→2050 target revision; PSFIG window; LWCF FY26 protection). All 43 prior-method gap findings preserved.

**Risk-pattern defense.** Retrofit drift through reformatting was the principal risk; the defense (calibrate uncertainty AGAINST prior-method content) operated throughout. Each MC-XX entry's "what it affects" field is the Pattern 12 binding scope.

**Cross-domain reciprocity verification.** D6 verified file (2026-05-11) reciprocal references confirmed: MC-03 / G6-SD4-02 (AHERA principal anchor); MC-06 (AWWA v. EPA LCRI defense); SD6 (Wissahickon/Heinz NWR conservation-partnership); MC-04 / MC-32 (EJ principal anchors); Synthesis Section 2 (Penn anchor-accountability five-dimensional framework). D9 verified file (2026-05-11) reciprocal references confirmed: SD7 / F9-SD7-06 / UV-D9-12 (PA SUT 2% Philadelphia allocation principal anchor); SD4 PILOET architecture.

**Standard 13.C v2.2 carve-out application.** Annotated throughout at first reference of each named individual in official capacity. Discipline maintained.

**Same-session auditing limitation acknowledgment.** This retrofit was executed in the admin-batch (WA3) window. Same-session auditing is structurally weaker than independent admin-window review. Future cycle should consider independent post-retrofit review where time permits.

**Word count discipline.** Total document ~57K words (subject to final count); per-SD word counts within target range; Standard 16.C application.

**Methodology absorption candidates surfaced for lock-pass evaluation.** Surfaced in Synthesis Section 5; all carry explicit open questions; no direction is treated as settled until lead confirms.
